# AYBKK Assistant Duty

For assistants in the Mysore room at AYBKK. Part 1 is the job. Part 2 is the count.

## Part 1: The assistant's role

You are a second pair of eyes and hands for the main teacher, so every student in a full room gets seen. Your real job is noticing who is here, who is missing, who is hurt and who is new, and telling the main teacher. The main teacher decides each student's practice; you help them do the postures they already have, safely, breath first. Touch is offered, never taken: ask first, use light hands, and a no is always fine. In Boonchu's words, adjustment is "necessary once in a while", and "the practice is still your own."

### Before class

**Do**

- On a 05:30 morning the door check-in starts around 05:15. Be in and ready before it opens. [Boonchu to confirm: what time assistants arrive, and on which days and batches they assist]
- Ask the main teacher where they want you today, and which students they keep for themselves.
- Read the morning teaching brief, so you know who is new, who is hurt and who is coming back. [Boonchu to confirm: are assistants in the teacher LINE group that gets the 05:00 teaching brief, and is the brief switched on]
- Greet people by name at the door. Noticing who is here is half the job.
- Show a first-timer where to wait and where the mat goes. Tell them the teacher will guide them step by step.
- If someone came without a booking, add them at the door with "+ Add someone who is here". [Boonchu to confirm: do assistants run the door check-in at /door, or only the teacher]
- If a student tells you about an injury or a pregnancy, tell the main teacher before practice starts.
- Dress simply and skip the perfume. You will be close to people all morning.

**Don't**

- Don't walk in with the students. The room should already be calm when they arrive.
- Don't start teaching a newcomer the whole sequence at the door. The teacher starts them from zero.
- Don't quote prices or sell passes. Prices change, so send those questions to the desk. [Boonchu to confirm: who answers price and pass questions in the morning]
- Don't make a fuss when someone comes back after a break. Just say good morning.
- Don't tell anyone their body type or dosha at the door.
- Don't carry your phone in the practice room during class.

### During Mysore

**Do**

- Listen to the breath first. You can hear who is struggling from across the room.
- Go where the main teacher sends you, and go straight away.
- Help each student with the postures they already have.
- When someone fights a bind with a red face and no breath, bring them back to a shape they can breathe in.
- Give fewer corrections as a student gets better, so they learn to feel it without you.
- Speak low and close, to one student at a time.
- Keep one eye on the whole room, even with your hands on one student.
- Notice who is missing today, and tell the main teacher after class.

**Don't**

- Don't give a new posture or take one away. That is the main teacher's call.
- Don't adjust everyone in every posture. Boonchu says adjustment is needed once in a while, and the practice stays their own.
- Don't adjust and walk off. Stay until the breath is back.
- Don't let the shape win over the breath.
- Don't chat with other assistants across the room. Use your eyes and small gestures.
- Don't count for a student or rush them. It is their pace and their breath.

### Adjustments: touch and consent

**Do**

- Ask before you touch, every time, even someone you adjusted yesterday.
- Say where your hand will go and why, in a few words.
- Look first, then touch. In public posts, students praise the teacher who "observes first" and is "never forceful".
- Start light and stable, on the big bones, so the student feels steadier.
- Use the breath as your stop sign: release, get the breath back, then think about depth.
- Check for a consent card or chip, and still ask out loud. [Boonchu to confirm: does AYBKK use consent cards or chips, and where are they kept]
- If fingers go tingly or numb in a bind, the student comes out at once. Keep your hands off that arm until the main teacher decides.
- Take a no as the full answer, and stay just as warm with that student.

**Don't**

- Don't force anyone into a posture, ever.
- Don't push someone deeper while they hold their breath for a bind.
- Don't touch a part of the body you haven't named first.
- Don't take yesterday's yes as today's yes. A student can say no at any point.
- Don't use your body weight, or any adjustment the main teacher hasn't taught you. [Boonchu to confirm: may assistants give dropback assists, and does AYBKK use the Mysore towel or top rule]
- Don't touch an injured or pregnant student until the main teacher says how, or whether.
- Don't make a student explain why they said no.
- Don't film or photograph an adjustment without the student's OK.

### Safety and injuries

**Do**

- If someone feels faint, lie them down with the legs raised, or sit them with the head low.
- If someone faints during practice, or doesn't come round within a minute, call 1669. [Boonchu to confirm: list 1646 (Bangkok) beside 1669? Only 1669 is confirmed by official sources]
- If someone is hot, dizzy or sick, move them somewhere cool, give water and cool the skin.
- Confusion, a seizure, or hot skin that has stopped sweating is an emergency. Call 1669.
- Watch knees in lotus and half lotus. If the knee hurts, they come out.
- Watch the neck in headstand and shoulderstand. The head stays still.
- Write down what you saw in plain words, and give it to the main teacher the same morning.
- Know where the first aid kit is, and the address to give: S31 Sukhumvit Hotel, Sukhumvit 31. [Boonchu to confirm: where the first aid kit is, the floor and room to give 1669, and who makes the call]
- An injured student still comes at the same hour. The main teacher gives them what the body can do today.

**Don't**

- Don't diagnose or name an injury, even when you're sure.
- Don't prescribe anything: no medicine, no diet, no treatment plan.
- Don't give anyone a recovery timeline.
- Don't offer to fix a sore shoulder after class. That is treatment, and we are not licensed for it.
- Don't soften the adjustment and carry on when a symptom runs down the arm. Stop and pass it on.
- Don't let someone who fainted get straight back up and practise.
- Don't leave an unwell student alone, and don't let them drive themselves to hospital.
- Don't wait until next week on numbness or night pain. It goes to the main teacher today.

### Respecting the main teacher

**Do**

- The main teacher decides each student's practice. We help them do it.
- If the main teacher steps in on your adjustment, step back and watch. Being corrected well is how an assistant earns trust.
- Use the main teacher's words for cues, and check anything you're unsure of after class.
- Pass on everything a student tells you about pain, injury, pregnancy or worry.
- When you don't know, say "I don't know yet, I'll ask the teacher."
- Talk to students about pace, depth and when to stop, the way the main teacher does.
- Save your questions for after class.

**Don't**

- Don't give a new posture without the main teacher.
- Don't tell a student to stop at a posture or skip ahead on your own.
- Don't correct the main teacher in front of a student.
- Don't make a line sound stronger than the teacher said it. When a student checks, it has to be true.
- Don't hand out labels like "you are a Vata".
- Don't argue when the main teacher sends you somewhere else. Just go.

### Shala etiquette

**Do**

- No phones, no photos, no talking during practice. That goes for us too.
- Know the moon days. The shala is closed on full moon and new moon days.
- Let students wait their turn, and bring them in when a spot is free. [Boonchu to confirm: how spots are given when the room is full]
- Respect a ladies' holiday. Traditionally women rest the first three days, and it is her choice. [Boonchu to confirm: AYBKK's guidance on ladies' holiday]
- Say "shala" in English. In Thai, write ชาลา.
- Move quietly: no running and no loud sounds in the room.
- Say "See you tomorrow" when students leave.

**Don't**

- Don't photograph or film students on your own phone.
- Don't ask a woman why she is resting or skipping inversions.
- Don't share student names, notes or numbers outside the team.
- Don't say "we miss you". Say "See you tomorrow" and expect them back.
- Don't eat or drink in the practice area. That is the rule at the Sharath Yoga Centre in Mysore.
- Don't step over or onto a student's mat. Walk around it.

### Closing the room

**Do**

- Make sure everyone does the finishing postures and takes rest, even when they're late for work.
- When a student reaches their last posture, send them to backbends if they have them, then to finishing.
- Keep the resting area quiet, and let people rest.
- Remind students to clean their mat and the floor around it with the spray.
- Check that everyone who practised is checked in before the class ends. After that, the door won't take a walk-in.
- Write your notes in the teaching notes: one thing to work on, and what the team should know. [Boonchu to confirm: do assistants get a passcode for the teaching notes page, or do they tell the main teacher]
- Before you leave, tell the main teacher who was missing, who got hurt and who was new.
- Close the room the way the main teacher shows you. [Boonchu to confirm: closing jobs: lights, fans or air, windows, props and mats, and who locks up]

**Don't**

- Don't start loud cleaning while people are still resting.
- Don't hurry anyone out of rest so you can close up.
- Don't leave "Let the student read the notes too" ticked on a team-only note. It is ticked by default.
- Don't write a diagnosis in the notes. Write what you saw, like "shoulder still guarding on the right".
- Don't message a missing student yourself. The team sends the "See you tomorrow" notes on set days. [Boonchu to confirm: may assistants message students, or does only the team send the 'See you tomorrow' notes]
- Don't leave before the main teacher is finished with the room.

## Part 2: Primary Series vinyasa count

### How to read the count

- A vinyasa is one movement with one breath.
- Count 1 starts from Samasthiti. Samasthiti itself has no number.
- The state of the asana is the posture itself: the count where you stay for the breaths.
- Totals are full vinyasa. The last count is the forward fold. The inhale back up to Samasthiti is not counted.
- In half vinyasa you go from Downward Dog straight into count 7 of the next posture (Kino MacGregor, Petri Räisänen).

### Where the numbers come from

- This sheet follows the Pattabhi Jois count in Yoga Mala. In his foreword to Yoga Mala (2009), Sharath writes: "The method explained in this book is identical to the method taught at his institute in Mysore, the method we follow today."
- Sharath's own count book, Ashtanga Yoga Anusthana, could not be found for this check. His led count is known only from second-hand notes. So no cell is checked against Sharath directly.
- Where Yoga Mala is silent (most gazes, most breath numbers), the cell shows Lino Miele's count book, Astanga Yoga. Where neither book has it, the cell or the flag names the source.
- Breath numbers like "5 breaths" are Lino Miele's. Yoga Mala usually says to breathe in the state "as much as possible", and its notes say 5 to 8 breaths is enough. This holds in every table, so it is not flagged row by row. Where Yoga Mala gives its own number or time, the cell shows it.
- Every other difference has a ⚑ marker and is explained under the table. Sharath's led count (second-hand) is in the flags where it differs.

### Surya Namaskara A (9 vinyasas)

| Count | Sanskrit | Movement | Breath | Drishti |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Ekam | From Samasthiti, arms up over the head, palms together, head back a little | Inhale | Fingertips (Yoga Mala) ⚑1 |
| 2 | Dve | Fold forward (Uttanasana), hands beside the feet, nose toward the knees | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 3 | Trini | Lift the head only, hands stay on the floor | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) |
| 4 | Chatvari | Jump back to Chaturanga Dandasana, body straight on hands and toes | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 5 | Pancha | Upward Dog (Urdhva Mukha Svanasana): chest forward, arms straight, thighs off the floor, tops of the feet down | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑2 |
| 6 | Shat | Downward Dog (Adho Mukha Svanasana): hips up, heels down, belly in | Exhale, 5 breaths ⚑3 | Nabi chakra (navel) ⚑3 |
| 7 | Sapta | Jump the feet between the hands, head up, back straight | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) |
| 8 | Ashtau | Fold forward (Uttanasana) | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 9 | Nava | Come up, arms over the head, palms together. Then arms down to Samasthiti | Inhale. Then an uncounted exhale to Samasthiti ⚑4 | Fingertips (Yoga Mala: as count 1) ⚑1. Samasthiti: Nasagrai (nose) ⚑4 |

- ⚑1: Counts 1 and 9. Lino Miele says thumbs (Angusta ma dyai). Yoga Mala's method says fingertips, but its own drishti paragraph says Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) for count 1. John Scott, Larry Schultz and ayi.info say thumbs. Sweeney says third eye.
- ⚑2: Lino Miele says Broomadhya, and Yoga Mala's rule (odd counts look between the eyebrows) agrees, but its method names no gaze here. Kino MacGregor says up, Larry Schultz says sky, and Sweeney's text says nose or third eye.
- ⚑3: The 5 breaths come from Lino Miele. Yoga Mala says only "hold position", with no number. Both books name the navel, but Yoga Mala's general rule (even counts look at the nose) gives nose, and Sweeney's chart shows nose. Sweeney's text calls the navel the standard.
- ⚑4: Neither lineage book numbers Samasthiti or gives its breath. The exhale back to Samasthiti comes only from secondary sources (John Scott, Kino MacGregor, Sweeney, ayi.info). Samasthiti gaze: Yoga Mala and Sweeney say nose; Kino MacGregor says "open". Larry Schultz numbers the return as step 10, which is not a vinyasa count.

### Surya Namaskara B (17 vinyasas)

| Count | Sanskrit | Movement | Breath | Drishti |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Ekam | Utkatasana: bend the knees (together), arms up, palms together | Inhale | Fingertips (Yoga Mala) ⚑1 |
| 2 | Dve | Straighten the legs, fold forward (Uttanasana) | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 3 | Trini | Lift the head, straighten the back | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) |
| 4 | Chatvari | Jump back to Chaturanga Dandasana | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 5 | Pancha | Upward Dog (Urdhva Mukha Svanasana) | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑2 |
| 6 | Shat | Downward Dog (Adho Mukha Svanasana), flowing on, no hold | Exhale | Nabi chakra (navel) ⚑3 |
| 7 | Sapta | Virabhadrasana A ⚑5: right foot forward between the hands, right knee bent, left leg straight back, arms up | Inhale | Fingertips (Yoga Mala) ⚑1 |
| 8 | Ashtau | Hands down, back to Chaturanga Dandasana | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 9 | Nava | Upward Dog | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑2 |
| 10 | Dasha | Downward Dog, flowing on, no hold | Exhale | Nabi chakra (navel) ⚑3 |
| 11 | Ekadasha | Virabhadrasana A ⚑5, left foot forward, arms up | Inhale | Fingertips (Yoga Mala: as count 7) ⚑1 |
| 12 | Dvadasha | Hands down, back to Chaturanga Dandasana | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 13 | Trayodasha | Upward Dog | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑2 |
| 14 | Chaturdasha | Downward Dog, held | Exhale, 5 breaths ⚑4 | Nabi chakra (navel) ⚑4 |
| 15 | Panchadasha | Jump the feet between the hands, head up | Inhale | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) |
| 16 | Shodasha | Fold forward (Uttanasana) | Exhale | Nasagrai (nose) |
| 17 | Saptadasha | Utkatasana: bend the knees, arms up. Then straighten the legs, arms down to Samasthiti | Inhale. Then an uncounted exhale to Samasthiti ⚑6 | Fingertips (Yoga Mala: as count 1) ⚑1. Samasthiti: Nasagrai (nose) ⚑6 |

- ⚑1: Counts 1, 7, 11 and 17 (arms up). Lino Miele says thumbs. Yoga Mala's method says "the tips of the fingers", and its odd-count rule gives Broomadhya. In Yoga Mala, 11 follows 7 and 17 follows 1. John Scott, Larry Schultz and ayi.info say thumbs. Sweeney says third eye.
- ⚑2: Upward Dog. Lino Miele says Broomadhya, and Yoga Mala's odd-count rule agrees. Kino MacGregor says up, Larry Schultz says sky, and Sweeney's text says nose or third eye.
- ⚑3: Downward Dog. Both books name the navel. Yoga Mala's general rule (even counts look at the nose) gives nose, and Sweeney's chart follows that rule.
- ⚑4: The 5 breaths come from Lino Miele, at count 14 only. Yoga Mala says 14 follows 6 ("stare at the navel") and gives no number. Yoga Mala's even-count rule and Sweeney's chart give nose.
- ⚑5: Lino Miele says only "Virabhadrasana, right leg" (and left leg at 11). Yoga Mala gives no name. The label "Virabhadrasana A" comes from secondary sources.
- ⚑6: Neither lineage book numbers Samasthiti or gives its breath. The exhale to Samasthiti comes only from secondary sources (John Scott, Kino MacGregor, Sweeney, ayi.info).

### Standing sequence

| Sanskrit name | Total vinyasa count | State of the asana (count) | Breath | Drishti |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Padangusthasana | 3 | 2 | Exhale in, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Pada Hastasana | 3 | 2 | Exhale in, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Utthita Trikonasana | 5 ⚑2 | 2 (right), 4 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Hastagrai (the raised hand) |
| Parivrtta Trikonasana | 5 ⚑3 | 2 (right), 4 (left) ⚑3 | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Hastagrai (the raised hand) |
| Utthita Parsvakonasana | 5 ⚑2 | 2 (right), 4 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Hastagrai (the hand over the ear) |
| Parivrtta Parsvakonasana | 5 ⚑3 | 2 (right), 4 (left) ⚑3 | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Hastagrai (the top hand) ⚑4 |
| Prasarita Padottanasana A | 5 | 3 | Exhale, head down, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Prasarita Padottanasana B | 5 ⚑5 | 3 | Exhale, head down, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Prasarita Padottanasana C | 5 ⚑6 | 3 | Exhale, head down, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Prasarita Padottanasana D | 5 | 3 | Exhale, head down, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Parsvottanasana | 5 | 2 (right), 4 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑7 |
| Utthita Hasta Padangusthasana | 14 (7 each leg) | Right: 2 (fold), 4 (leg to the side), 7 (leg in front, hands on hips). Left: 9, 11, 14 | 2, 4, 9, 11: exhale in. 7, 14: inhale in. 5 breaths each. Leg down on an uncounted exhale after 7 ⚑8 | 2, 9: Padhayoragrai (toes). 4: Parsva, look left. 11: Parsva, look right ⚑8. 7, 14: not confirmed ⚑8 |
| Ardha Baddha Padmottanasana | 9 ⚑9 | 1 and 2 (right), 6 and 7 (left) ⚑9 | 1, 6: inhale into the bind. 2, 7: exhale into the fold, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Utkatasana | 13 ⚑10 | 7 | Inhale in (jump the feet forward, bend the knees, arms up), 5 breaths | Fingertips (Yoga Mala) ⚑10 |
| Virabhadrasana A and B (one asana) | 16 ⚑11 | A: 7 (right), 8 (left). B: 9 (left), 10 (right) | 7 inhale, 8 exhale, 9 inhale, 10 exhale. 5 breaths each ⚑11 | A: fingertips (Yoga Mala) ⚑11. B: Hastagrai (left hand at 9, right hand at 10) |

- ⚑1: Yoga Mala names no gaze here, so the nose comes from Lino Miele. John Scott, Kino MacGregor, Sweeney and Larry Schultz agree. In Padangusthasana, John Scott looks between the eyebrows on the head-up counts 1 and 3.
- ⚑2: Lino Miele and the KPJAYI chart in his book list "Utthita Trikonasana A & B (5)" and "Utthita Parsvakonasana A & B (5)", where B is the revolved form. Read like the chart's "Prasarita Padottanasana (A,B,C,D) (5)", that means 5 for each part. Yoga Mala has only the Utthita form.
- ⚑3: Not in Yoga Mala. Lino Miele and the KPJAYI chart count it as part B under one heading of 5. John Scott gives it its own 5, from Samasthiti and back. Kino MacGregor goes straight on from the Utthita form and counts 2 to 5 again (ayi.info and von Deck do the same for Trikonasana). Sweeney counts on as 6 to 9, with the states at 6 and 8. Gaze, breath and 5 breaths a side agree.
- ⚑4: Lino Miele, John Scott, Kino MacGregor and Sweeney say the hand. Only Larry Schultz says sky.
- ⚑5: Yoga Mala, Lino Miele and John Scott give 5: count 5 opens the arms wide. The modern led count stops B at 4 and flows into C (Kino MacGregor, von Deck, ayi.info). Sweeney counts the arms-open move as both B's 5 and C's 1.
- ⚑6: Yoga Mala and Lino Miele both give 5, but differ on the arms. At counts 1 and 5, Yoga Mala has the hands on the waist; Lino Miele and John Scott open the arms. Kino MacGregor, von Deck and ayi.info give C 4 counts. Sweeney uses count 5 (arms wide) only in full vinyasa.
- ⚑7: Total and states agree everywhere. Gaze: Lino Miele says nose, and Sweeney agrees. John Scott, Kino MacGregor and Larry Schultz say the toes of the front foot. Yoga Mala names no gaze.
- ⚑8: Yoga Mala and Lino Miele agree on the total, the states and the breath. Look left at 4 is Yoga Mala's; look right at 11 comes from Kino MacGregor. Neither book names a gaze for 7 and 14; Lino Miele lists only "Parsva and Padhayoragrai" for the whole asana. For 7 and 14, John Scott and Kino MacGregor say toes; Sweeney says nose. Larry Schultz holds the leg-up state for 3 breaths, and ayi.info keeps the body upright at count 6.
- ⚑9: Yoga Mala names four states: the standing bind (1, 6) and the fold (2, 7). Lino Miele and John Scott hold the breaths only at the folds, 2 and 7. Sweeney does not count the leg-down exhale, so his count runs 1 to 8, with the folds at 2 and 6. Every source agrees on the gaze.
- ⚑10: Total and state agree in Yoga Mala, Lino Miele and John Scott. In the modern led count you skip 12 and 13 and go from 11 (Downward Dog) straight into Virabhadrasana count 7 (Kino MacGregor, ayi.info, von Deck; Sweeney writes the jump back as "9/4"). These are all secondary sources. Gaze: Yoga Mala points to Surya Namaskara B count 1, "the tips of the fingers". Lino Miele's Utkatasana page and John Scott say up (Urdhva). Lino Miele's own Surya B chart, Kino MacGregor, Larry Schultz and ayi.info say thumbs. Sweeney says the hand.
- ⚑11: Yoga Mala, Lino Miele and John Scott count A and B as one asana of 16. The modern led count starts it at 7 from Utkatasana's Downward Dog (Kino MacGregor: 14 movements). Breath: Lino Miele goes 8 inhale (after an uncounted exhale), 9 exhale, 10 inhale. Kino MacGregor's text, Sweeney and ayi.info follow Yoga Mala; John Scott mixes the two. Gaze in A: Lino Miele's asana page and John Scott say up; Lino Miele's Surya B chart, Kino MacGregor and Larry Schultz say thumbs; Sweeney says the hand. Kino MacGregor's appendix lists B right at 9 and left at 10, against her own text and every other source.

### Seated sequence

| Sanskrit name | Total vinyasa count | State of the asana (count) | Breath | Drishti |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dandasana | None of its own: count 7 of Paschimattanasana ⚑1 | 7 (inside Paschimattanasana) | Inhale, jump through and sit. Yoga Mala: breathe here as much as possible ⚑1 | not confirmed ⚑1 |
| Paschimattanasana A (big toes) | 16, one count for every grip ⚑2 | 9 | 8: take the feet (Yoga Mala: on an exhale) ⚑2. 9: exhale in, 5 breaths | Padhayoragrai (toes) ⚑2 |
| Paschimattanasana B (hands over the tops of the feet) ⚑3 | 16, same count as A | 9 | Exhale in, 5 breaths | Padhayoragrai (toes) |
| Paschimattanasana C (sides of the feet) ⚑3 | 16, same count as A | 9 | Exhale in, 5 breaths | Padhayoragrai (toes) |
| Paschimattanasana D (wrist beyond the feet) ⚑3 | 16, same count as A | 9 | Exhale in, 5 breaths | Padhayoragrai (toes) |
| Purvattanasana | 15 | 8 | Uncounted exhale to place the hands behind. Inhale up into 8, 5 breaths. 9: exhale, sit down | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑4 |
| Ardha Baddha Padma Paschimattanasana | 22 | 8 (right), 15 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Padhayoragrai (toes of the straight leg) ⚑5 |
| Tiryang Mukha Eka Pada Paschimattanasana ⚑6 | 22 | 8 (right), 15 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Padhayoragrai (toes of the straight leg) |
| Janu Sirsasana A | 22 ⚑7 | 8 (right), 15 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Padhayoragrai (toes of the straight leg) |
| Janu Sirsasana B (sitting on the heel) | 22 | 8 (right), 15 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Padhayoragrai (toes of the straight leg) |
| Janu Sirsasana C (toes on the floor, heel toward the navel) | 22 | 8 (right), 15 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Padhayoragrai (toes of the straight leg) |
| Marichyasana A (knee up, fold) | 22 | 8 (right), 15 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Padhayoragrai (toes of the straight leg) |
| Marichyasana B (half lotus, knee up, fold) | 22 | 8 (right), 15 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths each side | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑8 |
| Marichyasana C (knee up, twist) | 18 | 7 (right), 12 (left) ⚑9 | Inhale into the twist, 5 breaths each side ⚑9 | Parsva (side): look right on the right side, left on the left ⚑9 |
| Marichyasana D (half lotus, knee up, twist) | 18 | 7 (right), 12 (left) | Inhale into the twist, 5 breaths each side ⚑9 | Parsva (side), as in C ⚑9 |
| Navasana | 13 | 7 (8 is the lift-up between rounds) | Inhale in, 5 breaths a round. Yoga Mala: 3 to 6 rounds ⚑10 | Nasagrai / Padhayoragrai (Lino Miele prints both) ⚑10 |
| Bhujapidasana | 15 | 7 and 8 ⚑11 | 7: inhale, jump around the arms, cross the feet. 8: exhale, chin to the floor, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Kurmasana | 16, one asana with Supta Kurmasana ⚑12 | 7 ⚑12 | Inhale in (jump around the arms and lower down), 5 breaths | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑12 |
| Supta Kurmasana | 16, same count as Kurmasana | 9 (8 is binding the hands) ⚑13 | 8: exhale, bind the hands behind the back. 9: cross the feet, 5 breaths ⚑13 | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑13 |
| Garbha Pindasana | 14 ⚑14 | 8, the rolling (7 is the seated hold) ⚑14 | 7: seated hold (Lino Miele counts it 8: exhale in, 5 breaths). 8: rolling, exhale back, inhale forward ⚑14 | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Kukkutasana | 14 ⚑15 | 8 ⚑15 | Inhale up, 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Baddha Konasana A (straight-back fold) | 15 ⚑16 | 7 (sit upright, knees down, holding the feet) and 8 (fold) ⚑16 | 7: inhale. 8: exhale in, 5 breaths. 9: inhale, sit up ⚑16 | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Baddha Konasana B (rounded back) ⚑17 | 15 ⚑17 | 8 ⚑17 | Inhale, lift the chest. Exhale, round the back, crown toward the feet. 5 breaths | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑17 |
| Baddha Konasana C ⚑18 | 15 | Sweeney: 9 (upright sit). ayi.info: 8 (rounded fold) ⚑18 | Sweeney: inhale in, 5 breaths. ayi.info: exhale in, 5 breaths ⚑18 | Nasagrai (nose), Sweeney only ⚑18 |
| Upavistha Konasana A (fold) | 15 ⚑19 | 8 | Exhale in, chest and chin toward the floor, 5 breaths | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑19 |
| Upavistha Konasana B (balance, holding the feet) | 15 | 9 ⚑20 | Inhale, lift up into the balance, 5 breaths | Urdhva (up) |
| Supta Konasana | 16 | 8 | Inhale, legs up. Exhale, feet to the floor behind the head, take the big toes. 5 breaths ⚑21 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑21 |
| Supta Padangusthasana A (leg up, nose to knee) | 28 with the side leg; 20 front only ⚑22 | 9 (right), 17 (left) in the 28 count ⚑22 | 8: inhale, leg up, take the big toe. 9: exhale in, 5 breaths | Padhayoragrai (toe) ⚑22 |
| Supta Padangusthasana B (leg to the side) | 28 | 11 (right), 19 (left) | Exhale in, 5 breaths | Parsva (side): look left when the right leg is out, right when the left leg is out ⚑23 |
| Ubhaya Padangusthasana | 15 | 9 | Inhale, roll up holding the big toes, 5 breaths | Urdhva (up) ⚑24 |
| Urdhva Mukha Paschimattanasana | 16 ⚑25 | 10 | 9: inhale, roll up holding the sides of the feet. 10: exhale, chin to the shins, 5 breaths ⚑25 | Padhayoragrai (toes) ⚑25 |
| Setu Bandhasana | 15 | 9 | 8: exhale, crown of the head down, arms crossed over the chest. 9: inhale up, 5 breaths ⚑26 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑26 |

- ⚑1: Yoga Mala and Lino Miele count Dandasana as count 7 of Paschimattanasana, not as its own asana. Yoga Mala says to breathe here "as much as possible"; Lino Miele gives only one uncounted exhale, then the inhale to 8. A 5-breath hold is only in secondary sources (Kino MacGregor, John Scott, Petri Räisänen, ayi.info; Larry Schultz says 5 to 8). Neither lineage book names a gaze. Secondary sources split: nose (Kino MacGregor, Petri Räisänen, ayi.info, Larry Schultz) or toes (John Scott).
- ⚑2: Yoga Mala and Lino Miele count one asana of 16 for every grip: the grip is taken at 8 and the fold is at 9. At 8, Yoga Mala takes the feet on an exhale; Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, John Scott and Kino MacGregor inhale. Larry Schultz gives the nose as the gaze.
- ⚑3: The letters mean different grips in different books. Yoga Mala has three grips and no letters: big toes, sides of the feet, wrist beyond the feet. Lino Miele has A big toes, B hands over the tops of the feet, C sides, D wrist. Petri Räisänen has only A, B, C: his B is the sides and his C is the wrist. Kino MacGregor teaches only A and D, and her D also allows interlaced fingers or the outsides of the feet. Larry Schultz's C clasps the hands all the way round the feet. ayi.info says Pattabhi Jois stopped teaching B around 2000 and C around 2004. Sharath's own lettering was not found. Name the grip, not the letter.
- ⚑4: Lino Miele and Petri Räisänen say nose. Kino MacGregor, John Scott, Larry Schultz and ayi.info say between the eyebrows. Yoga Mala names no gaze.
- ⚑5: Larry Schultz says nose. Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Kino MacGregor, John Scott and ayi.info say toes.
- ⚑6: Only the name varies. Yoga Mala writes "Tiriangmukhaikapada", Lino Miele "Triang Mukhaekapada". Petri Räisänen says Pattabhi Jois called it Tiryang (horizontal), and that "Triang" is a common misspelling.
- ⚑7: Yoga Mala gives Janu Sirsasana as three types, each with 22 counts and states 8 and 15. Lino Miele and Petri Räisänen print one count for all three.
- ⚑8: Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Kino MacGregor, John Scott and Larry Schultz say nose. The ayi.info page says between the eyebrows, but its own chart says nose.
- ⚑9: There is no fold, so the state is 7, not 8. Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, John Scott (C) and ayi.info twist on the inhale. Kino MacGregor twists on an uncounted exhale after the jump-through inhale. Lino Miele says only "Parsva"; which way to look comes from Petri Räisänen, Larry Schultz and ayi.info.
- ⚑10: Count and state agree everywhere. Rounds: Yoga Mala 3 to 6; Petri Räisänen, John Scott and Kino MacGregor 5; Sweeney and ayi.info 3 to 5. Lino Miele gives no number of rounds. Gaze: toes in Petri Räisänen, John Scott and Kino MacGregor; nose in Sweeney.
- ⚑11: Yoga Mala names two states: 7 (feet crossed, arms straight) and 8 (chin to the floor). Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney, John Scott and Kino MacGregor hold only 8. The way out differs: Yoga Mala and Petri Räisänen come back up to the count 7 shape at 9, and Petri says there is no Tittibhasana at 9 or 10. Lino Miele's 9 is "head up, Tittibhasana", then Bakasana.
- ⚑12: Yoga Mala, Lino Miele and Petri Räisänen count Kurmasana and Supta Kurmasana as one asana of 16. John Scott counts the pair as 15. theyogashala.com puts Kurmasana at 8, not 7. Gaze: between the eyebrows in Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, John Scott and Sweeney (Petri also allows the nose); nose in Kino MacGregor.
- ⚑13: Supta Kurmasana is the second state of the same 16 count. The state is 9 in Yoga Mala, Petri Räisänen, Kino MacGregor, theyogashala.com and ayi.info, but 8 in Lino Miele, Sweeney and John Scott (bind and crossed feet in one count). Yoga Mala gives no breath for 9. Petri Räisänen enters 9 on an inhale; Lino Miele, Sweeney and ayi.info enter on an exhale. Gaze: Sweeney and Kino MacGregor say nose. Way out: inhale, sit up with the legs behind the head, then lift and exhale to Bakasana.
- ⚑14: Yoga Mala counts 14, and its state at 8 is the rolling; 7 is the seated hold, arms through, holding the ears. Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen and Sweeney count 15: hold at 8 for 5 breaths, roll at 9. Rolls: nine (Lino Miele, Sweeney, ayi.info), 5 to 9 (Petri Räisänen), 5 (theyogashala.com), "a full circle" (Yoga Mala). John Scott, Sweeney, Kino MacGregor and theyogashala.com run Garbha Pindasana and Kukkutasana as one count.
- ⚑15: Yoga Mala and Petri Räisänen count Kukkutasana on its own: 14, state at 8. Lino Miele counts 15, state at 9 (8 is lotus with the arms through). Sweeney, John Scott, Kino MacGregor and theyogashala.com put it at 9 inside Garbha Pindasana's count.
- ⚑16: Yoga Mala and Lino Miele use no letters. Yoga Mala names two states, 7 (upright) and 8 (fold). Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney and Kino MacGregor hold the straight-back fold at 8; Petri Räisänen, Sweeney and Kino MacGregor call it A. ayi.info and Larry Schultz call the upright sit A. The upright sit after the fold (9) is held 5 breaths in Lino Miele and Sweeney, 2 in Petri Räisänen. Kino MacGregor and theyogashala.com number B as 10, so their total comes to 17.
- ⚑17: Only secondary sources have a rounded-back B; Yoga Mala and Lino Miele do not. Petri Räisänen and Sweeney (who calls it optional) put it inside count 8, total 15. Kino MacGregor and theyogashala.com number it 10. ayi.info calls this rounded fold C, and its B is the straight-back fold.
- ⚑18: Only secondary sources have a C, and they mean two different postures. Sweeney's C is the upright sit at 9 (Lino Miele's "straight back, 5 breaths", which has no letter). ayi.info's C is the rounded fold that Petri Räisänen and Kino MacGregor call B. Yoga Mala, Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, John Scott and Kino MacGregor have no C. Better to drop the letter and name the posture.
- ⚑19: Gaze in the fold: between the eyebrows in Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen (nose as a second option) and John Scott; nose in Kino MacGregor and Sweeney. Kino MacGregor and theyogashala.com add an "inhale, look up" count before B, so they reach 16. Petri Räisänen says Pattabhi Jois did not want 16.
- ⚑20: The state is 9 in Yoga Mala, Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney and ayi.info. Kino MacGregor numbers it 10, because of the extra look-up count. Petri Räisänen notes it never had its own name "B".
- ⚑21: Count and state agree everywhere. Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen and Sweeney mark the entry to 8 as an inhale. Gaze: nose in Petri Räisänen, John Scott and Kino MacGregor; navel in Sweeney. Lino Miele prints nose and between the eyebrows; Petri Räisänen says the eyebrow gaze is for count 9, chin to the floor after rolling up.
- ⚑22: Yoga Mala describes two parts: front only, 20 counts, states 9 and 13; front and side, 28 counts, states 11 and 19. In its 28 count it does not name 9 and 17 as states. Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen and Sweeney use 28 and hold 9 and 17 for 5 breaths. In the 28 count, 13 and 21 are a second nose to knee with no hold. Gaze: toe in Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen and Sweeney; nose in Kino MacGregor and John Scott. Petri Räisänen lets beginners look at the nose.
- ⚑23: Count and states agree. Which way to look comes only from secondary sources (Petri Räisänen, Kino MacGregor, theyogashala.com, ayi.info), and they agree. Lino Miele, John Scott and Sweeney say only "side".
- ⚑24: Up in Lino Miele ("Antara", which he equates with up), Petri Räisänen, Sweeney and Kino MacGregor. John Scott says between the eyebrows.
- ⚑25: Yoga Mala, Lino Miele and John Scott count 16. Petri Räisänen counts 17, and Sweeney, Kino MacGregor and ayi.info also number the head-up after the state as 11, which gives 17. Yoga Mala has that inhale but gives it no number. Petri Räisänen's text says 5 breaths, his table 8 to 10. Sweeney allows nose or feet for the gaze.
- ⚑26: Count and state agree. Gaze: nose in Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney and John Scott; between the eyebrows in Kino MacGregor. theyogashala.com lifts on an exhale; Yoga Mala, Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney, Kino MacGregor and ayi.info lift on an inhale.

### Finishing sequence

| Sanskrit name | Total vinyasa count | State of the asana (count) | Breath | Drishti |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Urdhva Dhanurasana | 15 ⚑1 | 9 | Inhale up, 5 breaths. 3 times (secondary sources) ⚑1 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑1 |
| Drop backs (standing backbends) | Not counted ⚑2 | John Scott only: 6 (hands land), 7 (stand up) ⚑2 | Exhale down, inhale up ⚑2 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑2 |
| Paschimattanasana (after backbends) | Half vinyasa, counts 7 to 14 ⚑3 | 9 | Exhale in, 10 breaths ⚑3 | Padhayoragrai (toes) ⚑3 |
| Salamba Sarvangasana | 13 | 8 | Inhale in. Yoga Mala: hold 5, 10, 15, even 30 minutes ⚑4 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑4 |
| Halasana | 13 | 8 | Exhale in, 25 breaths ⚑5 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑5 |
| Karnapidasana | 13 | 8 | Exhale in, 10 breaths ⚑6 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑6 |
| Urdhva Padmasana | 14 ⚑7 | 9 ⚑7 | Exhale into lotus, 10 breaths ⚑7 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑7 |
| Pindasana | 14 | 9 ⚑8 | Exhale in, 10 breaths ⚑8 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑8 |
| Matsyasana | 13 ⚑9 | 8 | Exhale in, 10 breaths ⚑9 | Nasagrai / Broomadhya (Lino Miele prints both) ⚑9 |
| Uttana Padasana | 13 | 8 | Inhale in, 10 breaths ⚑10 | Nasagrai (nose) ⚑10 |
| Sirsasana A | 13 | 8 | Inhale up. Yoga Mala: start at 5 minutes, add 5 minutes at a time ⚑11 | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Sirsasana B (half headstand, legs level with the floor; also called Urdhva Dandasana) | Not counted separately: inside Sirsasana ⚑12 | 9 (Kino MacGregor, Sweeney) ⚑12 | Exhale down to level, 10 breaths, inhale back up ⚑12 | Nasagrai (nose), per Sweeney ⚑12 |
| Sirsasana leg raises | Not counted separately ⚑13 | ayi.info: 7 down, 8 up ⚑13 | Exhale legs down, inhale legs up ⚑13 | not confirmed ⚑13 |
| Balasana (rest after Sirsasana: buttocks on heels, head on the floor) | Not counted: a rest inside Sirsasana ⚑14 | n/a | Exhale the feet down, then rest 2 minutes (Yoga Mala) ⚑14 | not confirmed ⚑14 |
| Baddha Padmasana | 16 | 8 ⚑15 | Yoga Mala: breathe deeply, no number. Lino Miele: 10 breaths ⚑15 | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Yoga Mudra | Not counted separately: count 9 of Baddha Padmasana ⚑16 | 9 (of Baddha Padmasana) ⚑16 | Exhale, chin to the floor, 10 breaths ⚑16 | Broomadhya (between the eyebrows) ⚑16 |
| Supported arch in lotus (John Scott only) | Not counted ⚑17 | John Scott only: 9 ⚑17 | 10 breaths (John Scott) ⚑17 | Broomadhya (John Scott) ⚑17 |
| Padmasana | 14 (Lino Miele) ⚑18 | 8 ⚑18 | Exhale in, 25 breaths ⚑18 | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Utpluthih (Yoga Mala: Uth Pluthi) | 14 (Lino Miele) ⚑19 | 9 ⚑19 | Inhale up, 25 breaths ⚑19 | Nasagrai (nose) |
| Jump back and Samasthiti | The count ends at 14, then Samasthiti ⚑20 | n/a | 10: exhale, jump back to Chaturanga. 11: inhale, Upward Dog. 12: exhale, Downward Dog. 13: inhale, jump forward, head up. 14: exhale, fold. Uncounted inhale up to Samasthiti ⚑20 | Nasagrai (nose) |

- ⚑1: Not in Yoga Mala: its Primary ends at Setu Bandhasana and its finishing starts at Sarvangasana. Lino Miele lists it as the last Primary posture, with 15 counts (his page heading is misprinted 16). Lino Miele gives no number of repetitions. 3 times: John Scott, Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney, Larry Schultz; ayi.info says 3 to 5. Only Larry Schultz gives a different gaze, between the eyebrows.
- ⚑2: Not in Yoga Mala or Lino Miele. Only secondary sources describe drop backs, done with the teacher's help. Number: 3 (John Scott, Kino MacGregor) or 5 at Primary level (Sweeney). Petri Räisänen says advanced students may stand up after Urdhva Dhanurasana and keep backbending alone or with a teacher, with no count. The gaze comes from John Scott and Sweeney.
- ⚑3: Not counted as its own posture in Yoga Mala or Lino Miele. Only secondary sources put a Paschimattanasana here. The half vinyasa count 7 to 14 comes from Petri Räisänen, Kino MacGregor and ashtanga.lifestyle. Seated Paschimattanasana in full vinyasa is 16 (Yoga Mala, Lino Miele). Breaths: 10 (Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney, John Scott); Larry Schultz 5 to 15; ayi.info 5, with 10 or more invited. Gaze: toes (Kino MacGregor), nose (Larry Schultz). John Scott and Sweeney do it straight after the assisted backbends, with the teacher's help; ayi.info says Pattabhi Jois often took students straight into it.
- ⚑4: Count and state agree everywhere. Lying down first at count 7: Yoga Mala 4 to 5 deep breaths; Petri Räisänen and Kino MacGregor 5. The hold differs widely: Lino Miele, John Scott and Sweeney 25 breaths; Kino MacGregor 15 to 25; Petri Räisänen 10 to 15 (his chart 10 to 25); Larry Schultz 5 to 8, building to 25; Sharath's led class 10 (second-hand, ashtanga.lifestyle); ayi.info 10 in its heading, 5 in its text; Pattabhi Jois counted 5 in a 1997 demonstration (Grimmly blog). Gaze: Sweeney says navel; Larry Schultz says toes.
- ⚑5: Count and state agree. Lino Miele writes inhale at 8 because his page starts from lying down; the others exhale down from shoulderstand. Breaths: Lino Miele 25; John Scott and Sweeney 10; Petri Räisänen 8 to 10; Kino MacGregor and Sharath's led class (second-hand) 8; Larry Schultz 5 to 10; ayi.info 5. Sweeney alone says navel.
- ⚑6: Count and state agree. Lino Miele writes inhale, counting from lying down; the others exhale. Breaths: 10 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney); Petri Räisänen 8 to 10; Kino MacGregor 8; Larry Schultz 5 to 10; ayi.info 5. No source found for Sharath's number here. Sweeney alone says navel.
- ⚑7: Yoga Mala and Petri Räisänen count 14, with the state at 9 (8 is Sarvangasana). Lino Miele counts 13, with the state at 8. Entry breath: exhale in Yoga Mala, Petri Räisänen, John Scott, Larry Schultz and ayi.info; inhale in Lino Miele, Sweeney and Sharath's led count (second-hand, per Kino MacGregor and ashtanga.lifestyle). Breaths: 10 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney); Kino MacGregor 8; ayi.info 5. Sweeney says navel.
- ⚑8: Every source counts 14. The state is 9 in Yoga Mala, Petri Räisänen and John Scott; Lino Miele puts it at 8 and adds "release hands" at 9. Entry: Lino Miele writes inhale; the others exhale. Breaths: 10 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney); Kino MacGregor 8; ayi.info 5. Sweeney says navel.
- ⚑9: Yoga Mala and Petri Räisänen count 13, with Chakrasana as count 9. Lino Miele counts 14 and adds "release hands and feet" at 9. ayi.info enters on an inhale; the others exhale. Breaths: 10 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney); Kino MacGregor 8; ayi.info 5. Gaze: between the eyebrows in Kino MacGregor, John Scott and Larry Schultz; nose in Petri Räisänen and Sweeney. Petri Räisänen notes that Pattabhi Jois later asked for straight arms here.
- ⚑10: Count and state agree. Yoga Mala gives no entry breath. Inhale: Lino Miele, Sharath's led count (second-hand), ayi.info, Larry Schultz. Exhale: Petri Räisänen, Sweeney. Breaths: 10 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney); Kino MacGregor 8; ayi.info 5. Gaze: nose in Lino Miele, John Scott, Petri Räisänen and Sweeney; between the eyebrows in Kino MacGregor and Larry Schultz. Way out: Chakrasana into count 4 (Yoga Mala).
- ⚑11: Count, state and gaze agree. The hold differs a lot. Yoga Mala starts at 5 minutes and adds 5 at a time, toward 3 hours. Sharath's conference notes from 2011 (second-hand) speak of 10, 15, 20 minutes. Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney and Larry Schultz say 25 breaths; Kino MacGregor 15 to 25; Petri Räisänen 10 to 50; Sharath's led class 15 (second-hand); ayi.info 10; Pattabhi Jois counted 10 in a 1997 demonstration. Yoga Mala and Lino Miele teach one headstand only, with no B.
- ⚑12: Not in Yoga Mala or Lino Miele. It is given by Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle (Sharath's led class), Sweeney, Petri Räisänen and Larry Schultz, and Pattabhi Jois counted it in a 1997 demonstration (Grimmly blog). Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle and Sweeney number it 9; Petri Räisänen and ayi.info put it inside count 8. Breaths: 10 in most; John Scott 5, after 5 leg raises; Larry Schultz lowers on an inhale, 5 breaths. Gaze: nose (Sweeney), toes (Larry Schultz). ayi.info says Pattabhi Jois used both names.
- ⚑13: Only ayi.info, John Scott and Sweeney give leg raises. They are not in Yoga Mala, Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen, Kino MacGregor or ashtanga.lifestyle. ayi.info: 10 times. Sweeney: 5 times, after the half headstand. John Scott: 5 times, before it. No source names a gaze.
- ⚑14: Every source rests after headstand. Yoga Mala puts the rest inside Sirsasana, before count 9 (jump back); Petri Räisänen counts it as part of 9; Lino Miele leaves it out of the count. Length: 2 minutes (Yoga Mala, Lino Miele, John Scott); Petri Räisänen 1 to 2, and at least 2 after a headstand longer than 15 minutes; Sweeney 10 breaths; Kino MacGregor and Sharath's led class (second-hand) 5 uncounted breaths. Sharath's conference notes from 2012 (second-hand): come out slowly and rest without lifting the head. Gaze: Petri Räisänen says nose; Sweeney says eyes closed.
- ⚑15: Yoga Mala and Petri Räisänen put the state at 8, with Yoga Mudra as count 9. Lino Miele puts it at 9 (8 is taking lotus) and counts Yoga Mudra as its own asana. Entry: exhale in Petri Räisänen, John Scott, Sweeney, Kino MacGregor and ayi.info; inhale in Lino Miele and ashtanga.lifestyle. Breaths: 10 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney); ayi.info 5, and it says the breaths were later often dropped; Kino MacGregor 1; Petri Räisänen counts none and goes straight into Yoga Mudra.
- ⚑16: Yoga Mala, Petri Räisänen and Sharath's led count (second-hand) treat Yoga Mudra as count 9 of Baddha Padmasana (16). Lino Miele counts it as its own asana of 15, with the state at 8. Breaths: 10 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Kino MacGregor, Sweeney, Petri Räisänen); Larry Schultz 10 to 25; ayi.info 5. Gaze: between the eyebrows in Yoga Mala and every other source except Kino MacGregor (nose). Petri Räisänen allows the nose too.
- ⚑17: Only John Scott gives this arch in lotus after Yoga Mudra (he calls it "Panmasana"). He labels it count 9 (Nava), with 10 breaths and the gaze between the eyebrows. It is not in Yoga Mala, Lino Miele, Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle, ayi.info, Petri Räisänen, Sweeney or Larry Schultz.
- ⚑18: Yoga Mala gives no total. Petri Räisänen counts Padmasana and Utpluthih together as one asana of 14, with states 8 and 9. The led count numbers Padmasana 10, because it follows Yoga Mudra (Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle, Sweeney). Entry: exhale in Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen and Sweeney; inhale, coming up from Yoga Mudra, in Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle, John Scott and ayi.info. Breaths: 25 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney, Larry Schultz); Petri Räisänen 10 to 25; 10 (Kino MacGregor, ayi.info, and a slow 10 from Pattabhi Jois in 1997); Yoga Mala "as many times as possible". Hands in jnana mudra in Petri Räisänen, Sweeney, ashtanga.lifestyle and ayi.info; Yoga Mala only says hands on the knees.
- ⚑19: Yoga Mala gives no count and says it is "not an actual asana". Lino Miele, John Scott and Petri Räisänen count 14, with the state at 9. The led count numbers it 11 (Kino MacGregor; ashtanga.lifestyle says Pattabhi Jois counted it this way, and that Sharath's recordings also have 9 or 10). Breaths: 25 (Lino Miele, John Scott, Sweeney; Larry Schultz "25 fire breaths"); Petri Räisänen 10 to 25; Kino MacGregor 10 or more; ayi.info 10, and it says Pattabhi Jois taught 100 rapid breaths in the 1970s and 1980s. Gaze agrees everywhere, Yoga Mala included. The name Tolasana is not in any source checked.
- ⚑20: Lino Miele, Petri Räisänen and John Scott end the count at 14. The led count (Kino MacGregor, ashtanga.lifestyle) runs to 16, because it numbers Utpluthih 11: 12 jump back, 13 Upward Dog, 14 Downward Dog, 15 jump forward and look up, 16 fold, then inhale up to Samasthiti for the closing mantra. Then Ekam to Shat as in Surya Namaskara A, and at Sapta jump through, lie down and rest. Sweeney does a full vinyasa for the closing mantra, then jumps through to rest. Yoga Mala: jump back to count 4 of Surya Namaskara, do the usual vinyasas, then jump through, lie down and rest 5 minutes. Rest: Yoga Mala 5 minutes; Kino MacGregor 5 to 20; ashtanga.lifestyle 5 to 10; ayi.info at least 7; Larry Schultz 10 to 20.

# Yoga is easy (Boonchu's text, hook + double hook) · 20 Nov 2026
Lane B · Toolkit: Triple Hook (Contradiction) + Myth-Kill. Workflow: 4 writers (Triple Hook, Stakes-first, Gervais, Vibe Samurai) + 2 judges; both judges ranked the Triple Hook draft first.
His text: "Yoga is easy but you must spend time doing it. / If you want shortcut, go where there's many good practitioner / There must be some secret! / Or come to aybkk, you will know how."
Stakes: without time on the mat you keep starting over. Why care: busy people blame a stiff body; the real block is the calendar, and the room is the one part they can choose.
Script (v1, sent to Boonchu):

## v3 (Boonchu: "Your script is not a human talk.") — plain talk
Yoga is easy.
Really. The hard part is... you must spend time doing it.
And nobody has time, right? So we all look for a shortcut.
OK... there is one.
Go where there's many good practitioners.
You watch them, and you think... there must be some secret!
Yes, there is.
A teacher shaped them. Every morning, for years.
They didn't get there alone.
So come to AYBKK. You will know how.

## 2026-11-20 · carnegie-recovery-wellness-investment

# Recovery and wellness in Bangkok: investor report

For Boonchu, from Carne. 20 Nov 2026.

How to read this. Money is in THB, and USD is at 34 THB (33.94 on 20 Nov 2026 [S1]). A tag like [S2] links to a source. ESTIMATE means my own sum or range, with how I built it. EBITDA means profit before tax, interest and your own pay. Payback means the months until your cash comes back.

## 0. One-page summary

**Answer: do not put cash into a recovery business now.** One action: offer FORM a zero-cash student referral test for 90 days.

- Cheap recovery is crowded, and every studio format loses money when three normal shocks hit together (ESTIMATE, section 3).
- It breaks your own rule: "No equipment race... chains win there" [S3].
- FORM is not a machine studio. It sells therapist hours at 2,100 THB [S4]. That is a people business, like yours.

**5 key numbers**

| # | Number | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Thai wellness economy: US$46.5bn in 2025, up 8.8%. Growth has slowed each year since 2023. | [S5] |
| 2 | Bangkok ice bath and sauna visit: 200 to 890 THB. | [S2] |
| 3 | Best own studio (MID, 200 m2): 12.73M THB in, 3.75M EBITDA a year, 47 months payback. | ESTIMATE, section 3 |
| 4 | Same studio with price -20%, visits -30% and rent +30%: it loses 0.76M a year. | ESTIMATE, section 3 |
| 5 | USD 1M net a year needs about 9 such studios and 115M THB of cash. | ESTIMATE: 34M ÷ 3.75M |

**The 3 biggest risks**

1. Prices fall. Cheap clubs keep opening, and chains are adding recovery [S6], [S7].
2. Sunk cost (money you can never get back) on unproven demand. Building work is 56% of a SMALL studio's cash (ESTIMATE). No Bangkok venue publishes visit numbers.
3. Safety and law. A cryo chamber killed a worker in Las Vegas [S8]. A hyperbaric fire killed a child in Michigan [S9]. Officials raided a Bangkok clinic over unlicensed IV drips, reported 13 Nov 2026 [S10].

**The cheapest 90-day test (0 THB of capital)**

- Ask FORM for a student rate, with one tracked code per student. Teachers refer students they see are injured.
- Ask for the other direction too: FORM hands patients who finish physio a card with a keyed AYBKK link (aybkk.net/start/form). You already count those taps. A FORM patient is a new Bangkok student. Your students are only FORM's customers.
- Optional: a refundable 1,500 THB deposit list for a shala recovery add-on. Target: 22 deposits, the corner's break-even point (ESTIMATE).
- On day 90, check: do FORM users renew more than similar students?

**What to ask FORM**

1. The company record at the Department of Business Development: owners and filed accounts.
2. 24 months of profit and loss for each branch.
3. Unused prepaid wallet credit, which a buyer would owe.
4. Therapist hours sold versus hours open, and how often clients come back.
5. Licences and lease terms for each branch.
6. Why only 1 of the 3 sites announced in Sep 2025 has opened [S11], [S12].
7. Will FORM share a monthly count of how often AYBKK codes are used?

## 1. Market

| Measure | Number | Year | Kind | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thai wellness economy | US$46.5bn, +8.8%, 26th in the world | 2025 | FACT | [S5] |
| Same, in baht | about 1.53 trillion THB | 2025 | ESTIMATE: x 32.9, the 2025 average rate | [S13] |
| Growth by year | 15.3%, 16.9%, 11.6%, 8.8% | 2022 to 2025 | FACT | [S5] |
| Spa market (MarketLine) | 70.7bn THB, to 97.1bn by 2028 | 2025 to 2028 | ESTIMATE: paid report, second-hand | [S14] |
| Licensed health shops | 17,897 (16,609 massage, 1,092 spa) | Oct 2024 | FACT | [S15] |
| Foreign share of spa revenue | over 90%; 2026 growth only 4 to 5% | 2026 | FACT | [S16] |
| Thai fitness revenue | over 12bn THB, +18%; over 80% in Bangkok | 2025 | FACT | [S17] |
| Recovery studios in Thailand | no number published | | | Unclear spots |
| Contrast-therapy studios, world | US$381.4m, about 18% a year | 2025 | ESTIMATE: vendor teaser | [S18] |
| Monthly wellness spend | over half spend 1,000 to 3,000 THB; 1 in 4 spend more | late 2025 | FACT: Watsons members only | [S19] |

The market is big and still growing. But it leans on tourists, and growth is slowing. No one measures recovery in Bangkok, so every studio plan has to guess its demand.

**Main players.** Gym chains are moving in. Fitness First has 32 clubs and about 60,000 members, and its 2027 flagship adds "wellness and recovery" [S6]. Jetts has 64+ clubs [S20]. GymNation opens in Q1 2027 at 999 THB a month [S7].

Price ladder (ESTIMATE, from this table): clubs 200 to 890 a visit, clinics 900 to 3,500, medical 2,800 to 6,000. Most places sell visits and packs, not monthly habits.

Your price as an anchor (ESTIMATE). The AYBKK 1-month pass is 10,272 THB with VAT [S3]. That buys 4.9 FORM physio hours, 13.7 RECHILL visits, or 3.7 BDMS cryo sessions.

## 2. What you need

| IV drips | chairs, clinic kit | not priced here | | needs a doctor and clinic licence | [S10] |

Nitrogen for a cabin costs about 22,000 to 27,000 THB a month (ESTIMATE: 2020 US gas prices x 34 [S39]). Kit totals (ESTIMATE, sums of these prices): value 0.53M THB, mid 2.68M, premium 8.0M. They do not include freight, duty, VAT or installation. Anyone can buy the same kit, so equipment is not an edge.

**Space.** My model uses 100 m2 (SMALL), 200 m2 (MID), 400 m2 (PREMIUM) and 15 m2 (shala corner). These are ESTIMATES.

| Rent | THB per m2 a month | Date | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thonglor and Ekkamai shophouses, asking | 175 to 1,389; most single floors 250 to 750 | Nov 2026 | [S40] |
| Grade A office, central | 1,037 | Q4 2025 | [S41] |
| Prime mall retail (JLL) | 2,670 | Q4 2025 | [S42] |

**Location** (ESTIMATE). Pick a ground floor on a Sukhumvit soi near the BTS, not a mall. Mall rent is 3 to 10 times higher. Rivals sit on Soi 20, 24, 27, 32 and 49 [S2], [S12]. AYBKK on Soi 31 is in the same area.

**Building work.** Bangkok office fit-out costs USD 91 per sq ft in 2026 [S43]. That is about 33,300 THB per m2 (ESTIMATE: x 10.764 x 34). Wet rooms usually cost more. Get 3 Thai quotes.

**Power.** Under 30 kW peak, business power costs 3.25 to 4.42 THB per kWh [S44]. Bigger new connections pay 210 THB per kW of peak demand. On top of that they pay 4.33 THB per kWh at peak times and 2.64 at other times [S44]. Add the fuel charge of 0.1623 per kWh [S45] and 7% VAT.

| Staff | THB a month | Date | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bangkok minimum wage | 400 a day | from Jul 2025 | [S46] |
| Front desk | 13,000 to 22,000 | Nov 2026 | [S47] |
| Floor attendant | 12,000 to 13,800 | Nov 2026 | [S48] |
| Massage therapist | 15,000 base + fees per treatment | Nov 2026 | [S49] |
| Physiotherapist | 20,000 to 40,000 | Nov 2026 | [S50] |
| Clinic nurse | 35,000 to 65,000 | Nov 2026 | [S51] |
| Doctor | no rate found | | |

Thai massage work is for Thai nationals only [S52]. A Health Ministry adviser put the shortage of massage workers at about 70,000 [S53].

### Thai licences

| Service | Licence needed | Kind | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sauna + plunge + massage + 3 listed extras | spa licence (Health Establishment Act 2016) | rule FACT; fit ESTIMATE | [S54], [S55] |
| Sauna, plunge, compression, red light, no massage | probably outside that Act; district bath and steam permit; may not say "spa" | ESTIMATE | [S56], [S57] |
| Physiotherapy | licensed physio inside a licensed clinic | FACT, secondary | [S58] |
| IV drips | medical clinic licence and a doctor | FACT | [S10] |
| Hyperbaric; cryo with health claims | no Thai ruling found; treat as medical until the Thai FDA says no | ESTIMATE | [S59] |

The 3 extras come from a list of 29. The list includes sauna, steam, cold compress, yoga and meditation [S55].

| Item | Number | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Spa licence by floor area | 1,000 (up to 100 m2); 3,000; 6,000; 10,000 (over 400 m2) | [S60] |
| Yearly fee; operator licence | 1,000; 1,000 | [S60] |
| No spa licence | up to 6 months in jail, 50,000 fine, or both | [S61] |
| Saying "spa" with no licence | fine up to 40,000 | [S57] |
| Clinic approval | 3 to 6 months; 0.5M to 2M THB legal cost | ESTIMATE: law-firm range [S62] |
| Clinic with no licence | up to 5 years and 100,000 | [S10] |
| Medicine with no licence | up to 3 years and 30,000 | [S10] |
| VAT | register above 1.8M a year; 7% to 30 Sep 2027; clinic care exempt | [S63] |

Plain steps for a spa format:

1. Call the Department of Health Service Support (DHSS) on 1426. Ask for your category in writing.
2. Ask the district office about change of use, fire rules and the bath and steam permit [S56].
3. Hire a licensed operator: 20 or older, with a DHSS certificate and a passed DHSS test [S54]. The operator must be on site in every opening hour [S57].
4. Register every therapist with DHSS. DHSS decides within 60 days [S64].
5. Apply for the spa licence. Meet the safety rules: trained staff, clocks and thermometers, an automatic emergency stop, and health screening [S65].
6. Buy liability and workers' compensation insurance. A US cryo site was shut 7 days after a death because it could not prove it had that cover [S66].
7. Never claim a service treats or prevents disease [S57].

Every number in this section is an ESTIMATE from my model. Its inputs are the prices, rents, wages and fees in sections 1 and 2. VAT is taken out of revenue.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| Other lines | | massage 1,200 an hour | physio 2,100; cryo 1,800; hyperbaric 4,000; IV 4,500 | | at or below listed prices [S4], [S23], [S24], [S25] |

The model assumes 60% of visits are by members, at 6 visits a month each.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| Equipment | 1.50 | 3.17 | 12.42 | 0.71 |
| Building work | 3.33 | 6.66 | 16.00 | 0.50 |

Building work costs more than the machines. Restore, the top US brand, shows the same pattern. Its building work is USD 325k to 600k, and its equipment is USD 169k to 205k [S67].

| Running cost (THB a month) | SMALL | MID | PREMIUM | Corner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Rent | 50,000 | 120,000 | 555,600 | 0 |
| Staff | 54,375 (3) | 207,135 (11) | 573,555 (18, with a guessed doctor at 150,000) | 14,490 (1) |
| Power | 24,216 | 55,315 | 93,993 | 4,715 |
| Insurance, software, admin, upkeep | 28,507 | 58,839 | 167,099 | 7,549 |
| **Fixed total** | **157,098** | **441,289** | **1,390,246** | **26,754** |

| Results (base) | SMALL | MID | PREMIUM | Corner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Base volume | 18 visits a day | 35 visits + 8 massage hours a day | 30 visits a day + medical services | 30 subscribers + 100 single uses a month |
| Takings a month, with VAT | 313,200 | 1,017,750 | 2,542,200 | 75,000 |
| EBITDA a year | 1.04M | 3.75M | 6.08M | 0.41M |
| EBITDA margin | 30% | 33% | 21% | 49% |
| Break-even | 11.6 visits a day | 20.5 visits + 4.7 massage hours a day | 73% of base volume | 22 subscribers |
| Payback from opening | 86 months | 47 months | 94 months | 42 months |
| Cash back after 5 years | 0.61x | 1.22x | 0.52x | 1.30x |

Payback starts when the doors open, so add 2 to 4 months of building work. Treat MID's 33% margin as the good case. US studios report less: Perspire 18.4% net [S68] and Degree Wellness 30% [S69]. The median US fitness club makes 23.6% EBITDA [S70].

| What if: EBITDA a year | Base | Price -20% | Visits -30% | Rent +30% | All three | 9% franchise fees |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SMALL | 1.04M | 0.46M | 0.16M | 0.86M | **-0.43M** | 0.73M |
| MID | 3.75M | 1.80M | 1.04M | 3.32M | **-0.76M** | 2.73M |
| PREMIUM | 6.08M | 1.40M | -0.75M | 4.08M | **-6.02M** | 3.44M |
| Corner | 0.41M | 0.27M | 0.19M | 0.41M | **+0.09M** | 0.34M |

These shocks are normal. Cheap rivals open almost every month [S2]. Restore shrank from 230 to 212 sites between 2023 and 2025 [S71].

**Scale check.** Your goal is USD 1M net a year, about 34M THB. It would take 32.6 SMALL studios (193.5M THB of cash), 9.1 MID (115.3M) or 5.6 PREMIUM (230.1M). These are all ESTIMATES, before tax and head-office costs. US results point the same way: about 5.5 to 9 studios (ESTIMATE from [S68], [S69]).

| Risk | Evidence | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Price war | entry from 200 THB; new venues almost every month | [S2] |
| Chains add recovery | Fitness First flagship; GymNation at 999 a month | [S6], [S7] |
| Trend fades | Restore: 31 franchise closures in 2024 and 2025 | [S71] |
| Deaths and injury | cryo suffocation; hyperbaric fire; collapse after 10 minutes in an ice bath | [S8], [S9], [S72] |
| Law | IV clinic raid, reported 13 Nov 2026 | [S10] |
| Your attention | a 12-month ramp-up beside the 180K course | ESTIMATE |

**The science is thin.**
- Whole-body cryo: very low quality, 4 trials with 64 people [S73].
- Cold plunge: moderate evidence for less soreness 24 hours after hard exercise, but no strength gain [S74]. After lifting, it may slightly reduce muscle gain [S75].
- Compression: a small effect [S76].
- Red light: no effect on top strength in 14 trials [S77].
- Sauna: promising, but only 3 of 13 trials were at low risk of bias [S78].

## 4. Options

| Option | Cash in | Base EBITDA a year | All 3 shocks | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A. Start your own | 5.93M to 41.17M | 1.04M to 6.08M | all lose money | No |
| B. Buy a franchise | none on offer in Thailand | MID 2.73M after fees | worse than A | No |
| C. Stake in FORM | unknown | your share of an unknown profit | unknown | No cash until you see data |
| D1. Referral with FORM | about 0 | small | 0 | Yes, test it |
| D2. Shala corner | 1.33M | 0.41M | +0.09M | Only if the test passes |

**A. Start your own.** MID is the best format, and the answer is still no. Building work is sunk, and visit numbers are guesses. PREMIUM also needs a doctor and clinic licences at a time when enforcement is active [S10].

**B. Buy a franchise.** A franchise means paying a brand for its system and name.
- No US recovery brand is for sale in Thailand.
- °CRYO already has a Bangkok operator [S79]. Its only public cost figure is USD 550k to 750k, from 2021 (ESTIMATE [S80]).
- Restore charges a 7% royalty plus a 2% brand fund [S67]. Fees of 9% push MID payback from 47 to 71 months (ESTIMATE).
- Check every franchise claim. In 2026 the US FTC made the Club Pilates franchisor return USD 17M to franchisees [S81].

**C. Stake in FORM.**

- What it is: a clinic for physio, Chinese medicine and applied Thai traditional medicine [S4], [S82]. Its machines are compression boots, red light and a laser add-on [S4]. It has 4 sites open and 2 "coming soon" [S12]. No owners or revenue are published.
- How it earns: say a physio on 30,000 plus 15% commission sells 6 hours a day. That costs about 507 THB an hour. About 76% of the 2,100 price is left for rent, overhead and profit (ESTIMATE).
- A fair price: small private healthcare service firms sell for 2.7 to 3.9 times yearly EBITDA. That range is for firms whose key staff often leave (ESTIMATE, US-weighted study [S83]).
- Never pay more than it would cost to build the same profit yourself. MID costs 12.73M for 3.75M a year, about 3.4 times (ESTIMATE).
- Formula: your share x (2.7 to 3.9 x real EBITDA, minus debt and unused wallet credit, plus cash).
- A small stake gives you no control. Ask for monthly accounts, a veto on new debt and new sites, and the right to sell on the founders' terms.

**D. Fit with AYBKK.**

- D1, referral both ways. Zero cash. Students go to FORM. FORM's patients who finish physio come to the shala through aybkk.net/start/form, which the site already counts. The second direction is the one that makes you money. Your course already teaches teachers to "refer the medical question out" [S84]. One kept 12-month member is worth 59,900 THB plus VAT [S3]. Take a student rate, not a referral fee, until a lawyer checks the clinic rules.
- D2, recovery corner. One sauna, one plunge, two sets of boots and one trained attendant in 15 m2 (ESTIMATE). It is the only format that survives all 3 shocks. But 0.41M a year is about 2.3 seats of the 180K course. If you give it free to members, it pays for itself only if 7 to 13 students a month stay who would have quit (ESTIMATE).
- No source shows that a sauna or plunge helps keep members. One chain's survey shows interest only [S85]. In a 2026 US survey, 53% of lapsed gym members just drifted away, and about half were never contacted [S86].

**The conflict with your written strategy.**

- Your rule: "No equipment race (cold plunge, sauna, reformers): chains win there. We win on habit and teacher." [S3]
- Your shala page, habit 7: "Heat from inside, cooled by your breath. Your own warmth, no machine." Habit 6: "Calm you train, not buy." [S87]
- Your 180K pitch is "how to build students who stay for years" [S3]. A machine corner tells those buyers that kit keeps students.
- The evidence backs your rule. If you ever build a corner, rewrite habit 7 first.

**Hormozi value equation.** Value = (dream outcome x how likely it works) ÷ (time to result x effort and cost).

| Part | Recovery studio | AYBKK |
|---|---|---|
| Dream outcome | recover, sleep, feel calm: wanted, but the same everywhere | stronger, calmer, better sleep |
| How likely it works | low to medium: thin science; Thai law bans treatment claims [S57] | an authorised teacher; proof checked at weeks 0, 6 and 12 [S87] |
| Time to result | good: you feel it in the session | slower: weeks |
| Effort and cost | high: travel, cold, a price every visit | same time, same place, a teacher who notices |

Every studio buys the same kit and faces the same limits on claims. So the only way a studio can win is by cutting effort, which means being where people already are. Chains do that at scale.

**Munger inversion.** Ask how this would fail, then avoid it.

1. Pay for building work before demand is proven.
2. Fight on price in a tier that starts at 200 THB.
3. Add IV drips or a doctor while enforcement is active.
4. Skip oxygen checks, grounding or insurance, or let anyone plunge alone.
5. Buy a stake, or a franchise, on numbers no one has checked.
6. Take your eyes off the 180K course for a year.

Avoid all 6 and you are left with D1.

## 5. Recommendation

**Do not put cash into recovery now. This week, offer FORM a zero-cash student referral test.**

Stakes: 5.9M to 41M THB of largely sunk cash, chasing a goal it cannot reach.

Why care: your edge is a teacher who notices. A referral uses that edge, and a sauna fights it.

One small sum: 34M THB goal ÷ 3.75M per MID studio = 9.1 studios, about 115M THB of cash. One more 180K cohort of 10 students brings in 1.8M THB with no building work [S3]. (Cohort costs are not modelled.)

The honest weak point in my own answer: the 180K course hits a time wall. You can't teach 15 cohorts a year. A studio scales with cash, not your hours, and that is the only real case for it. But your graduates already scale without cash: they teach, and they invite you to workshops with 50+ students. That is people you trained, inside what you know. A sauna is not (my view, ESTIMATE).

| Day | Step | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| 1 to 3 | Draft the note to FORM: student rate, tracked codes, monthly count, the questions in section 0 | Carne drafts; Boonchu sends |
| 7 to 14 | Meet FORM. Agree the rate and a free 45-minute talk on practice injuries at the shala | Boonchu |
| 14 | Brief teachers: refer a student when you notice an injury | Boonchu |
| 14 | Make the aybkk.net/start/form card for FORM's front desk | Neo makes the tag; Plato makes the card; Boonchu says go |
| 14 | Optional: open a refundable 1,500 THB deposit list | Boonchu decides; front desk runs it |
| 30 and 60 | Check code use; send reminders | Machi |
| 90 | Compare renewals of FORM users with similar students, from the bkk_* tables | Neo pulls data; Carne writes it up |
| 90 | Decide, using the kill rules | Boonchu |

Kill rules (my thresholds, ESTIMATE):

- FORM will not share code counts: drop any stake idea.
- Fewer than 22 deposits: drop the corner and refund everyone.
- FORM users do not renew more: keep the referral as a service only.
- Fewer than 5 first bookings from aybkk.net/start/form in 90 days: FORM's patients are not your buyers. Stop asking FORM for anything more.

| We know (FACT) | We think (ESTIMATE) | We may be wrong about |
|---|---|---|
| Visits cost 200 to 890 THB [S2] | MID makes 3.75M a year at 35 visits a day | visit numbers: no Bangkok data |
| FORM sells therapist hours at 2,100 [S4] | a fair FORM price is 2.7 to 3.9 times EBITDA | FORM's real profit and owners |
| IV and physio need licensed clinics [S10], [S58] | a corner with no massage needs no spa licence | DHSS may class it differently |
| Chains are adding recovery [S6] | prices will keep falling | demand may grow faster than supply |
| Your strategy says no equipment race [S3] | a referral may lift renewals | no source proves it yet |

The plan in one sentence: keep your cash in the 180K course, and test a zero-cash FORM referral for 90 days.

**Shall I draft the note to FORM for you to send, yes or no?**

### Unclear spots (what could not be verified)

- No market size for recovery, cryo, plunge, sauna clubs or IV drips in Thailand or Bangkok. Only global vendor teasers.
- No Bangkok count of licensed spas. The government dataset returned a 403 error. National counts run from 11,700 to 17,900, depending on the source.
- No Bangkok venue publishes visit numbers or a monthly membership price.
- FORM: no owners, funding, revenue or company name found.
- Not published: prices at The Ice Bath Club, CRYO Thailand and the Fast & Fit monthly pass.
- No Bangkok doctor's pay rate. The model uses 150,000 a month as a placeholder.
- No Thai price for liquid nitrogen, sauna room builds or wet-room fit-out. Import duty was not researched.
- No Thai FDA ruling on cryo or hyperbaric chambers. The full text of the Health Establishment Act did not load.
- Whether a clinic may pay a referral fee was not researched.
- No study shows that a sauna or plunge helps a gym or yoga studio keep members.
